Sheinbaum continued the centralization of executive authority by dissolving seven autonomous agencies in late 2024: the [Federal Telecommunications Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Telecommunications_Institute "Federal Telecommunications Institute") (IFT), the [Federal Economic Competition Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comisi%C3%B3n_Federal_de_Competencia_Econ%C3%B3mica "Comisión Federal de Competencia Económica") (COFECE), the [National Institute for Transparency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Transparency_for_Access_to_Information_and_Personal_Data_Protection "National Institute of Transparency for Access to Information and Personal Data Protection") (INAI), the Energy Regulatory Commission (CRE), the National Hydrocarbons Commission (CNH), the [National Council for the Evaluation of Social Development Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_for_the_Evaluation_of_Social_Development_Policy "National Council for the Evaluation of Social Development Policy") (CONEVAL), and the National Institute for the Evaluation of Education (MejorEdu).[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[219\]](#refnote-222) Their functions were transferred to various cabinet ministries in what the administration described as an effort to streamline government operations and reduce public spending, though critics argued that the measure undermined transparency, regulatory independence, and checks on executive power.[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[220\]](#refnote-223)[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[221\]](#refnote-224)[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[222\]](#refnote-225)

On 1 April 2025, Sheinbaum published a constitutional reform to prohibit immediate reelection and bar close relatives of incumbents from running for the same office; although initially set for 2027, its implementation was postponed to 2030 following negotiations within the ruling coalition in the Senate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[223\]](#refnote-226)[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[224\]](#refnote-227)[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[225\]](#refnote-228) In February 2026, Sheinbaum proposed a broader electoral reform package to reduce the size of the [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_the_Republic_(Mexico) "Senate of the Republic (Mexico)"), modify proportional representation, cut party financing and electoral costs, eliminate the preliminary results program (PREP), and regulate the use of artificial intelligence in campaigns;[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[226\]](#refnote-229)[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[227\]](#refnote-230) however, the proposal failed to obtain a two-thirds majority after coalition allies—the [Ecologist Green Party of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecologist_Green_Party_of_Mexico "Ecologist Green Party of Mexico") (PVEM) and the [Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Party_(Mexico) "Labor Party (Mexico)") (PT)—withheld support amid concerns that the changes would weaken smaller parties, reduce their political influence, and concentrate power in the ruling party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[228\]](#refnote-231)

#### Protests and social unrest

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=33 "Edit section: Protests and social unrest") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=33 "Edit section's source code: Protests and social unrest")\]

The [Coordinadora Nacional de Trabajadores de la Educación](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinadora_Nacional_de_Trabajadores_de_la_Educaci%C3%B3n "Coordinadora Nacional de Trabajadores de la Educación") (CNTE) organized repeated strikes, work stoppages, and protest encampments, pressuring the government to fulfill campaign promises regarding the repeal of the 2007 ISSSTE Law and the improvement of pension benefits.[&#8202;](#ref-back-232-0)[\[229\]](#refnote-232)[&#8202;](#ref-back-233-0)[\[230\]](#refnote-233) In the fall of 2025, widespread farmer strikes and highway blockades erupted across multiple states, with agricultural workers and truckers demanding higher guaranteed crop prices, improved highway security, and the rejection of a water reform bill promoted by the government; these actions frequently paralyzed transport routes leading into Mexico City.[&#8202;](#ref-back-234-0)[\[231\]](#refnote-234) In November 2025, a [series of demonstrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mexican_protests "2025 Mexican protests") opposing the administration—sparked in part by the assassination of Uruapan mayor Carlos Manzo—took place in several cities; Sheinbaum characterized the protests as politically motivated and lacking organic support.[&#8202;](#ref-back-235-0)[\[232\]](#refnote-235)[&#8202;](#ref-back-236-0)[\[233\]](#refnote-236)

#### Welfare

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=34 "Edit section: Welfare") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section's source code: Welfare")\]

Sheinbaum expanded federal social programs including the introduction of the Women's Wellbeing Pension ( _Pensión Mujeres Bienestar_), which provides bimonthly financial assistance to senior women aged 60 to 64,[&#8202;](#ref-back-237-0)[\[234\]](#refnote-237)[&#8202;](#ref-back-238-0)[\[235\]](#refnote-238)[&#8202;](#ref-back-239-0)[\[236\]](#refnote-239) and the launch of the House to House Health ( _Salud Casa a Casa_) program, which delivers home-based medical care to elderly people and individuals with disabilities.[&#8202;](#ref-back-240-0)[\[237\]](#refnote-240) She also renamed the Benito Juárez Scholarship ( _Beca Benito Juárez_) to the Rita Cetina Gutiérrez Universal Scholarship ( _Beca Universal Rita Cetina Gutiérrez_), expanding it to provide bimonthly financial aid to all families with children enrolled in the public [basic educational system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Mexico#Structure_of_the_basic_education_system "Education in Mexico").[&#8202;](#ref-back-241-0)[\[238\]](#refnote-241) On 2 December 2024, Sheinbaum elevated several social programs to constitutional law.[&#8202;](#ref-back-242-0)[\[239\]](#refnote-242) Her administration has continued to prioritize social spending in budget allocations, with social program spending equivalent to approximately 3% of gross domestic product, according to the 2026 budget.[&#8202;](#ref-back-243-0)[\[240\]](#refnote-243)

Sheinbaum proposed a constitutional reform providing that annual minimum wage increases may not be set below the inflation rate; while the reform had not yet been enacted,[&#8202;](#ref-back-244-0)[\[241\]](#refnote-244) wage policy has been implemented in line with this framework, with the daily minimum wage set at MX$278.80 and later increased to MX$315.04 for 2026, consistent with a stated objective of reaching the equivalent of 2.5 [food baskets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_basket "Market basket") by 2030.[&#8202;](#ref-back-245-0)[\[242\]](#refnote-245) The administration also formalized the _[Ley Silla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ley_Silla "Ley Silla")_, granting service-sector workers the right to seating and rest periods,[&#8202;](#ref-back-246-0)[\[243\]](#refnote-246) enacted a reform regulating digital platform work that extends [IMSS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Social_Security_Institute "Mexican Social Security Institute") social security coverage and mandatory occupational accident insurance to active platform workers,[&#8202;](#ref-back-247-0)[\[244\]](#refnote-247) and reached a tripartite agreement to gradually reduce the legal workweek from 48 to 40 hours beginning in 2027.[&#8202;](#ref-back-248-0)[\[245\]](#refnote-248)

### Foreign policy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Foreign policy") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section's source code: Foreign policy")\]

Main article: [Presidency of Claudia Sheinbaum §Foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Claudia_Sheinbaum#Foreign_policy "Presidency of Claudia Sheinbaum")

See also: [List of international presidential trips made by Claudia Sheinbaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Claudia_Sheinbaum "List of international presidential trips made by Claudia Sheinbaum")

Sheinbaum's presidency has been dominated by relations with the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States") under the [second administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_presidency_of_Donald_Trump "Second presidency of Donald Trump") of [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump"). After Trump threatened tariffs of 25% on Mexican goods in early 2025, she negotiated repeated pauses and secured exemptions for goods that complied with the [United States–Mexico–Canada Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93Mexico%E2%80%93Canada_Agreement "United States–Mexico–Canada Agreement").[&#8202;](#ref-back-npr-pause2-0)[\[246\]](#refnote-npr-pause2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-newsweek-tariffs-0)[\[247\]](#refnote-newsweek-tariffs) Her government condemned the United States designation of Mexican drug cartels as [foreign terrorist organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State_list_of_Foreign_Terrorist_Organizations "United States Department of State list of Foreign Terrorist Organizations"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-npr-sovereignty-0)[\[248\]](#refnote-npr-sovereignty) and she rejected Trump's offer to send United States troops into Mexico, saying that "sovereignty is not for sale".[&#8202;](#ref-back-hill-troops2-0)[\[249\]](#refnote-hill-troops2) At the same time, Mexico expanded cooperation, transferring dozens of imprisoned cartel figures to the United States[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbc-0)[\[250\]](#refnote-bbc) and sharing intelligence in operations against cartel leaders, including the killing of [CJNG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalisco_New_Generation_Cartel "Jalisco New Generation Cartel") leader ["El Mencho"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nemesio_Oseguera_Cervantes "Nemesio Oseguera Cervantes") in 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fox-mencho-0)[\[251\]](#refnote-fox-mencho) Sheinbaum also opposed United States military action abroad as contrary to international law, objecting to a [campaign of strikes on suspected drug-trafficking boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_strikes_on_alleged_drug_traffickers_during_Operation_Southern_Spear "United States strikes on alleged drug traffickers during Operation Southern Spear") in international waters[&#8202;](#ref-back-mnd-boats-0)[\[252\]](#refnote-mnd-boats) and condemned [a United States military operation in Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_intervention_in_Venezuela "2026 United States intervention in Venezuela") in January 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-mnd-venezuela2-0)[\[253\]](#refnote-mnd-venezuela2)

Sheinbaum has sought to deepen Mexico's engagement with Latin America, calling for greater regional economic integration at the 2025 [CELAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_of_Latin_American_and_Caribbean_States "Community of Latin American and Caribbean States") summit[&#8202;](#ref-back-mnd-celac-0)[\[254\]](#refnote-mnd-celac) and aligning with other left-led governments.[&#8202;](#ref-back-democrata-gpm-0)[\[255\]](#refnote-democrata-gpm) She has maintained close ties with [Cuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuba "Cuba") through oil shipments and later humanitarian aid.[&#8202;](#ref-back-opis-cuba2-0)[\[256\]](#refnote-opis-cuba2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-pd-aid2-0)[\[257\]](#refnote-pd-aid2) Mexico's relations with [Ecuador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecuador "Ecuador") remained severed following the [2024 raid on its embassy in Quito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_raid_on_the_Mexican_embassy_in_Quito "2024 raid on the Mexican embassy in Quito"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-aol-ecuador2-0)[\[258\]](#refnote-aol-ecuador2) and broke down with [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru "Peru") in November 2025 after Mexico granted asylum to a former Peruvian prime minister, prompting Peru's Congress to declare Sheinbaum [persona non grata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persona_non_grata "Persona non grata").[&#8202;](#ref-back-ajz-peru-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-ajz-peru)

Sheinbaum has supported a two-state settlement of the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict "Israeli–Palestinian conflict") and called for recognition of a Palestinian state alongside Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-jpost-palestine2-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-jpost-palestine2) She condemned the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war") and, in September 2025, described Israel's actions there as a " [genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide "Gaza genocide")".[&#8202;](#ref-back-latimes-genocide2-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-latimes-genocide2)

### Approval rating

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=36 "Edit section: Approval rating") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section's source code: Approval rating")\]

Main article: [Opinion polling on the Sheinbaum presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Sheinbaum_presidency "Opinion polling on the Sheinbaum presidency")

Throughout her first year in office, Sheinbaum maintained high public approval ratings, consistently polling above 70%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-:5)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-:7) Between January and April 2025, her approval rose to approximately 80%, a surge that several analysts described as a [rally 'round the flag effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rally_'round_the_flag_effect "Rally 'round the flag effect") following her initial handling of tariffs imposed by U.S. President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-1)[\[263\]](#refnote-:7)[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-267) By December 2025, surveys by _Enkoll_ and _[El Financiero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Financiero "El Financiero")_ indicated that her approval had moderated to approximately 70%, coinciding with heightened public concern over violence in states such as Michoacán and broader economic uncertainty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-268)[&#8202;](#ref-back-269-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-269)[&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-270) Despite this decline, her disapproval rating remained comparatively low, at about 24%, and her approval continued to exceed that of her predecessor, López Obrador, at the same point in his presidency.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:5-1)[\[262\]](#refnote-:5)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-:6)

Demographically, Sheinbaum’s strongest support has been recorded among older adults, with approval ranging from 72% to as high as 86% in late 2025. This demographic is frequently cited as the primary beneficiary of the administration's expanded social programs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-1)[\[268\]](#refnote-:6)[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-272) Support has been lower among younger voters, with approval among those aged 18–24 polling between 57% and 66%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:6-2)[\[268\]](#refnote-:6)[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-273)

## Political views

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Political views") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section's source code: Political views")\]

### Social issues

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=38 "Edit section: Social issues") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section's source code: Social issues")\]

Sheinbaum is a self-described [feminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism_in_Mexico "Feminism in Mexico"), aligning her beliefs and actions with the principles of gender equality and women's rights.[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-274) She advocates for the legalization of [abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion "Abortion"), aligning her stance with broader movements aimed at promoting reproductive rights and autonomy for women.[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-275) During her leadership in Mexico City, Sheinbaum implemented a gender-neutral policy regarding school uniforms in state-run schools and championed [LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights "LGBT rights").[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-276) In 2022, she became the first head of government of Mexico City to attend the [city's gay pride march](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City_Pride "Mexico City Pride").[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-277)

### Economy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Economy") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section's source code: Economy")\]

Sheinbaum has criticized the [neoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberal "Neoliberal") economic policies of past presidents of Mexico, arguing that they have contributed to inequality in the country.[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-278) She has promised to expand welfare under her presidency[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-279) and intends to continue programs started by López Obrador, such as universal pension.[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-280)

### Environment

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Environment") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section's source code: Environment")\]

Sheinbaum has a background in environmental policy, having served as Minister of the Environment for Mexico City and worked on the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC),[&#8202;](#ref-back-cnn-profile-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-cnn-profile) which would go on to win the panel a [Nobel Peace Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize "Nobel Prize").[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-282) In her tenure as Minister of the Environment, she saw a marked reduction in air pollution and created community ecological reserves.[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-283) She has both spoken in favor of clean energy and support of oil, praising [PEMEX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PEMEX "PEMEX") (the nation's state-owned oil company).[&#8202;](#ref-back-sherman-profile-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-sherman-profile)

## Personal life

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Personal life") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section's source code: Personal life")\]

In 1986, Sheinbaum met [Carlos Ímaz Gispert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_%C3%8Dmaz "Carlos Ímaz"), who later became a prominent political figure in the PRD during his tenure at [Stanford University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_University "Stanford University"). They married in 1987 and divorced in 2016.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marriages-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-marriages) They have a daughter who was born in 1988.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marriages-1)[\[282\]](#refnote-marriages)

In 2016, she began dating [Jesús María Tarriba Unger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jes%C3%BAs_Mar%C3%ADa_Tarriba "Jesús María Tarriba"), a financial risk analyst for the [Bank of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Mexico "Bank of Mexico"), whom she had known as a university student.[&#8202;](#ref-back-dating-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-dating)[&#8202;](#ref-back-marriages-2)[\[282\]](#refnote-marriages)
In November 2023, Sheinbaum announced her marriage to Tarriba via social media.[&#8202;](#ref-back-dating-1)[\[283\]](#refnote-dating) The two married in a small civil ceremony.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marriages-3)[\[282\]](#refnote-marriages)

### Other awards

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=46 "Edit section: Other awards") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=46 "Edit section's source code: Other awards")\]

- Jesus Silva Herzog Award (1995). Awarded by the National Autonomous University of Mexico's Institute for Economic Research for her outstanding contributions in the field of economics.[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-291)
- UNAM Prize for Young Academics (2001). Awarded for her contributions to academia while working at the National Autonomous University of Mexico.[&#8202;](#ref-back-allblogthings.com-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-allblogthings.com)
- The Sustainability Medal (2024). Awarded by the Nobel Sustainability Trust[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-293) for her global leadership in sustainability and climate change policies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-294)
- Sheinbaum was part of the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) team that received the [Nobel Peace Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Peace_Prize "Nobel Peace Prize") in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Nobel_Peace_Prize "2007 Nobel Peace Prize") for their work on advancing the understanding of man-made climate change.[&#8202;](#ref-back-allblogthings.com-1)[\[289\]](#refnote-allblogthings.com)

## Selected bibliography

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&veaction=edit&section=47 "Edit section: Selected bibliography") \| [edit source](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Claudia_Sheinbaum&action=edit&section=47 "Edit section's source code: Selected bibliography")\]

Sheinbaum is the author of over 100 articles and two books on energy, the environment, and [sustainable development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_development "Sustainable development").[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-295) A selection follows:

- _Consumo de energía y emisiones de CO2 del autotransporte en México y Escenarios de Mitigación_, Ávila-Solís JC, Sheinbaum-Pardo C. 2016.
- Decomposition analysis from demand services to material production: The case of CO2 emissions from steel produced for automobiles in Mexico _, Applied Energy, 174: 245–255,_ Sheinbaum-Pardo C. 2016.
- The impact of energy efficiency standards on residential electricity consumption in Mexico _, Energy for Sustainable Development, 32:50–61_ Martínez-Montejo S.A., Sheinbaum-Pardo C. 2016.
- Science and Technology in the framework of the Sustainable Development Goals, World Journal of Science _, Technology and Sustainable Development, 14:2 – 17_. Imaz M. Sheinbaum C. 2017.
- Assessing the Impacts of Final Demand on CO2-eq Emissions in the Mexican Economy: An Input-Output Analysis _, Energy and Power Engineering, 9:40–54_, Chatellier D, Sheinbaum C. 2017.
- Electricity sector reforms in four Latin-American countries and their impact on carbon dioxide emissions and renewable energy _,_ Ruíz- Mendoza BJ, Sheinbaum-Pardo C. _Energy Policy_, 2010
- Energy consumption and related CO2 emissions in five Latin American countries: Changes from 1990 to 2006 and perspectives _,_ Sheinbaum C, Ruíz BJ, Ozawa L. _Energy_, 2010.
- Mitigating Carbon Emissions while Advancing National Development Priorities: The Case of Mexico, C Sheinbaum, O Masera, _Climatic Change_, Springer, 2000.
- Energy use and CO2 emissions for Mexico's cement industry, C Sheinbaum, L Ozawa, _Energy_, Elsevier, 1998.
- Energy use and CO2 emissions in Mexico's iron and steel industry, L Ozawa, C Sheinbaum, N Martin, E Worrell, L Price, _Energy_, Elsevier, 2002.
- _New trends in industrial energy efficiency in the Mexico iron and steel industry_, L Ozawa, N Martin, E Worrell, L Price, C Sheinbaum, [OSTI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSTI "OSTI"), 1999.
- Mexican Electric end-use Efficiency: Experiences to Date, R Friedmann, C Sheinbaum, _Annual Review of Energy and the Environment_, 1998.
- Incorporating Sustainable Development Concerns into Climate Change Mitigation: A Case Study, OR Masera, C Sheinbaum, _Climate Change and Development_, UDLAP, 2000.
